I mean, I’ve had rough days before, like anyone, but I can’t think of another stretch where it felt like this for this long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But when I’m lying awake before an early shift, I’ll think… maybe it’d be easier if I just didn’t wake up, so I wouldn’t have to keep feeling like I’m failing at everything.\n\nDoctor: If the mortgage situation were resolved, do you think you’d still feel this depressed?\n\nPatient: I think I would, yeah. The mortgage makes it worse, but most days there’s just this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I’m at work carrying plates and smiling because I’m supposed to, but it doesn’t feel like there’s anything good behind it.\n\nDoctor: Have you tended to avoid social situations because you were afraid people would judge or reject you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I haven’t been going out much, but it’s not because I’m scared people will judge me. I just don’t feel up to it most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this depression is mainly a reaction to the mortgage situation?\n\nPatient: Not mainly, no. The mortgage definitely adds to it, but this feeling is there most days anyway — like a heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I’m just going through the motions, even at work, smiling and carrying plates because I have to, not because I really feel anything good.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

But when I’m lying awake before an early shift, I’ll think… maybe it’d be easier if I just didn’t wake up, so I wouldn’t have to keep feeling like I’m failing at everything.\n\nDoctor: If the mortgage situation were resolved, do you think you’d still feel this depressed?\n\nPatient: I think I would, yeah. The mortgage makes it worse, but most days there’s just this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I’m at work carrying plates and smiling because I’m supposed to, but it doesn’t feel like there’s anything good behind it.\n\nDoctor: Have you tended to avoid social situations because you were afraid people would judge or reject you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I haven’t been going out much, but it’s not because I’m scared people will judge me. I just don’t feel up to it most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this depression is mainly a reaction to the mortgage situation?\n\nPatient: Not mainly, no. The mortgage definitely adds to it, but this feeling is there most days anyway — like a heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I’m just going through the motions, even at work, smiling and carrying plates because I have to, not because I really feel anything good.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a clear pattern with it. Some nights are fine, some aren’t, but I can’t say much more than that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s changed some. My eating is kind of all over the place. I keep thinking I let my family down by taking on this mortgage, and then every little mistake I make at work feels like proof that I’m useless… like I deserve whatever happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to enjoy joking around with the regulars at work, and I’d usually text my brother after my shifts. Lately I just kind of keep a straight face, say what I need to say, and leave it at that. And messages just sit there because I don’t really feel like answering.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days I feel pretty low. It’s like this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I still go to work and carry plates and smile when I’m supposed to, but it doesn’t really feel like there’s anything behind it.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start after taking on the mortgage?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it started after I took on the condo mortgage. It’s been going on for about a year and eight months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it excessive. I mean, I worry about stuff sometimes, but I haven’t really noticed myself worrying about a lot of different things all the time.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, had you ever gone through another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: Not really sure. I mean, I’ve had rough days before, like anyone, but I can’t think of another stretch where it felt like this for this long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But when I’m lying awake before an early shift, I’ll think… maybe it’d be easier if I just didn’t wake up, so I wouldn’t have to keep feeling like I’m failing at everything.\n\nDoctor: If the mortgage situation were resolved, do you think you’d still feel this depressed?\n\nPatient: I think I would, yeah. The mortgage makes it worse, but most days there’s just this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I’m at work carrying plates and smiling because I’m supposed to, but it doesn’t feel like there’s anything good behind it.\n\nDoctor: Have you tended to avoid social situations because you were afraid people would judge or reject you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I haven’t been going out much, but it’s not because I’m scared people will judge me. I just don’t feel up to it most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this depression is mainly a reaction to the mortgage situation?\n\nPatient: Not mainly, no. I’m just going through the motions, even at work, smiling and carrying plates because I have to, not because I really feel anything good.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a clear pattern with it. My eating is kind of all over the place. Some weeks I barely touch the staff meal at work, and then other nights I get home and just eat whatever’s in the kitchen, almost like I’m trying to settle myself down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energized or euphoric for several days and were not your usual self?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I’ve been feeling that a lot. I keep thinking I’ve let my family down with this mortgage, and then if I make even a small mistake at work, it feels like proof that I’m just useless. I’ve had rough patches before, but I don’t remember a clear time where it was nearly every day for two weeks like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. Some days I’m pacing around, checking the same tables or receipts over and over, and other times I’m just slow—like even grabbing menus or walking to the POS takes me longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you usually been a perfectionistic person who gets very upset when things aren’t done exactly right?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not usually. I like things done right, especially at work, but I wouldn’t say I’ve always been someone who falls apart if it’s not perfect.\n\nDoctor: Has this depressed mood been present continuously for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, not two years continuously. It’s been more like… about a year and a couple months that I’ve had these depressed, guilty periods.\n\nDoctor: At any time before this current period, did you ever have a separate episode of depression that lasted at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t remember a separate time before this where I was depressed for two full weeks like that. Nothing that clearly stands out to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I haven’t been going out much, but it’s not because I’m scared people will judge me. I just don’t feel up to it most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this depression is mainly a reaction to the mortgage situation?\n\nPatient: Not mainly, no. The mortgage definitely adds to it, but this feeling is there most days anyway — like a heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I’m just going through the motions, even at work, smiling and carrying plates because I have to, not because I really feel anything good.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a clear pattern with it. Some nights are fine, some aren’t, but I can’t say much more than that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s changed some. My eating is kind of all over the place. Some weeks I barely touch the staff meal at work, and then other nights I get home and just eat whatever’s in the kitchen, almost like I’m trying to settle myself down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energized or euphoric for several days and were not your usual self?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, but I can’t remember a stretch of several days where I was really energized or euphoric and acting unlike myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I’ve been feeling that a lot. I keep thinking I’ve let my family down with this mortgage, and then if I make even a small mistake at work, it feels like proof that I’m just useless. Like I deserve whatever comes from it.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed like this nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had rough patches before, but I don’t remember a clear time where it was nearly every day for two weeks like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. People at work have noticed it too. Some days I’m pacing around, checking the same tables or receipts over and over, and other times I’m just slow—like even grabbing menus or walking to the POS takes me longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you usually been a perfectionistic person who gets very upset when things aren’t done exactly right?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not usually. I like things done right, especially at work, but I wouldn’t say I’ve always been someone who falls apart if it’s not perfect.\n\nDoctor: Has this depressed mood been present continuously for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, not two years continuously. It’s been more like… about a year and a couple months that I’ve had these depressed, guilty periods.\n\nDoctor: At any time before this current period, did you ever have a separate episode of depression that lasted at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t remember a separate time before this where I was depressed for two full weeks like that. Nothing that clearly stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Before this current depression, did you ever have another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had bad days before, like anyone, but I can’t remember another period before this where I was depressed almost every day for two weeks or more."
